# Decision index

Keyed one-liners. The STORY lives where the pointer says — dev/NOTES.md
(section title), SEMANTICS.md (beside this file), tests/fidelity/divergences.md (row id),
dev/PROCESS.md — this file never duplicates it. Code comments cite keys as
`[D:key]` plus a half-line local why; dates live here, not in comments.
F#-border decisions REUSE their divergence-row id as the key (one name
per decision). Append-only: a reversed decision gets a new entry naming
the old key, never an edit-in-place.

| key | date | decision | story |

| self-module | 2026-07-27 | Script/process introspection grouped under a `Self` module (uppercase, the module convention), and `pid` added — freeing the bare names `args`/`stdin`/`scriptPath` for users. Origin: dogfooding — a script needs its own PID and wrote `$(sh -c 'echo $PPID') |> Seq.head`, a shell-out for a value the runtime knows. NAME `Self` (D1): the deciding argument is what the module ACCUMULATES — every plausible member (pid/args/stdin/scriptPath, later startTime/tty/cwd) is a fact about the running thing; `Script` is slightly wrong (pid is a process fact), `Proc` collides with the internal spawn vocab and Proc.fs, `Runtime` is accurate but lifeless. MEMBER `scriptPath` not `path` (D1b): the qualifier DISAMBIGUATES (script's path vs weir's exe vs cwd — two are things a script wants), it is not stutter (`Self.selfPid` would stutter; `scriptPath` says WHICH path, which the module name does not); `sourcePath` rejected because `source` already means source-code in the docs, and the clincher — the name matches the BOUNDARY that governs it: these members are SCRIPT-only, so concept, restriction, and error all say 'script'. CLEAN BREAK (D2): bare names removed, no aliases — a stale bare `args` is an unbound-var error whose did-you-mean (the existing bare-name->module-member teacher, Check.fs) points at `Self.args`; the migration teaches itself, as the casing rename did. Caveat: `args` has real muscle memory (every example) — expect a few days of the teaching error; fine, phase-scoped [D:drop-reify-builtins], no retirement machinery. `pid` (int) = Environment.ProcessId, stable across reads (the property fuzz.weir's lock liveness depends on; a per-read query would be a subtle bug); command use is `$"{Self.pid}"` (argv is strings; the interpolation hole converts inline — the string-vs-int question was weighed, string's payoff was one `show` and interpolation erases even that, so int stayed). FEASIBILITY: module members resolve to values via mangled `Module.member` keys, so a value member is no different from a function member; injected per-run in baseEnvs (both envs + strict copy) as a Modules entry + mangled Values. THE RE-VERIFIED FINDING (proven with a pin, not a grep, per the plan): `Args.load` gates script-mode on the introspection presence — it was `Map.containsKey 'args' env.Values`, now `Map.containsKey 'Self' env.Modules` (it reads Session.ScriptArgs at eval, so this is purely the availability gate). Env.load unaffected. Corpus migrated: repo-report `match Self.args`, fuzz.weir deletes its `$PPID` workaround (`acquire $"{Self.pid}"`), SKILL/GUIDE/SEMANTICS. | Tests 'the Self module' (types + teaching) + argvEnv on the Self module; e2e Self.pid stable-positive-int + scriptPath migrated; SEMANTICS script-inputs entry |
| doc-comments | 2026-07-28 | `///` doc comments (PLAN-doc-comments half 1). Attach to the declaration IMMEDIATELY below (a contiguous run accumulates in order; a blank OR a plain `//` breaks it). STORED OUT-OF-BAND, keyed by the physical (line,col,len) of the documented NAME — never by name (shadowing / inner lets / duplicate field names). The deciding argument (user, at review): this is NOT a divergence from the plan's "AST carries them / typed tree mirrors" — it is BETTER on the project's invariant-by-architecture-over-pin criterion (5th instance: one-pipeline, emergent-bound, one-spawn, capture-views). Erasure becomes a property of the REPRESENTATION: Value/Eval/Check never see a doc, so byte-identical runtime is trivial and there is NOTHING TO PIN — a doc is metadata about a source LOCATION, not part of the program's meaning; threading it through every AST/typed-tree constructor would add a field no arm reads (the binder-spans churn was paid for a SEMANTIC reason — spans feed the resolver; docs have none). Hover: TYPE FIRST, then the doc, at all five positions (top-level let, `type` decl, record field, union case, inner let) — `declHover` renders the type-decl structure / a field's type / a union case's signature for the KType position; let + inner-let already carried types. Completion items carry the doc (name-keyed index — a completion item IS a name, last-wins on a shared name). Alignment is a LINT, not the attribute machinery: docs are INERT (dropped before assembly) so a misaligned one cannot mis-parse — the `>]`-dangle precedent (a misaligned entry surfaced a misleading RUNTIME error) is about PARSE-affecting lines and does not apply; `Script.docMisalignments` errors both ways (doc above a field, doc above a union case) from analyzeLines, so it surfaces in `weir check` AND the LSP. fmt canonicalizes each `///` to its following declaration's formatted anchor (transparent to assembly, so the safety re-check holds; idempotent). Coloring: a distinct `comment.line.documentation` scope in all three grammars (TextMate name, tree-sitter `#match ^///` last-wins, micro `comment.doc` region before `//` — RE2 needs no lookahead, so not exempt); inventory 24->25. PARKED: param docs (a `/// - param x:` convention is structured-doc design — a bigger question; params already show their types on hover). RESIDUE (stated, chosen not defaulted): if docs ever leave the LSP (a `weir doc` command), the same pass serves, but a typed consumer wanting "the doc for this resolved symbol" goes through the position key, not the node. Half-2 doc-test answer: keep the Doc opaque markdown; half 2 embeds ```weir fences inside `///` and extends `ci/skill-doc.sh` with a `///`-aware pass (reuses the fence + runner), making builtin examples the project's only rot-proof docs. | NOTES: "/// doc comments — half 1" |

| modules-v1 | 2026-07-29 | USER modules & imports, arc SESSION 1 (design: dev/plans/DESIGN-modules-and-imports.md). Landed: the `module [Name]` marker (top-level, no `=`/body; reserved word) and `import "path" [as Name]` (literal-string path only; uppercase alias) as new `Stmt` cases; single-hop, CLI only. RESOLUTION is one function (`resolveImportPath` importing-file + path -> absolute normalized, symlinks UNRESOLVED per the Path.glob precedent); a missing import names the RESOLVED ABSOLUTE path. A module is checked in its OWN base env (`moduleBaseEnvs`: builtins-strict + prelude + Self, Self.scriptPath = its own path — PURE, so loading never disturbs the entry's process facts); its contributions merge as `Modules[alias]` (values + union ctors, so `alias.member` resolves the existing qualified way), `Types` (flat, PLAIN NAME so signatures/field-access/bare-literals all resolve with ZERO scheme-rewriting), and a new `TypeEnv.ModuleTypes: alias -> type-name set` (provenance for the qualified literal). TYPE REPRESENTATION — the load-bearing choice: plain-name keying (not qualified `Git.Ctx` keys) makes the common case (a shared record used in the module's own helper signatures) work with no rewriting; the cost is a SESSION-1 collision guard — an imported type name clashing with an existing type is a check error (cross-module same-name types are not yet distinguishable; qualified type names are a later session). NAMED / QUALIFIED RECORD LITERAL (`Ctx { .. }` / `Git.Ctx { .. }`, decision 7): NO new AST node and NO grammar change — the checker INTERCEPTS `EApp(typeName, ERecord)` (and `EApp(EField(module, type), ERecord)`), which is safe because a record type name is never a VALUE, so interception fires only where a bare application would already ERROR — `Some { .. }` (ctor applied to a record) is byte-identical (zero movement, pinned). Bare `{ .. }` field-set inference is unchanged; the named form disambiguates a two-record field-set collision. DECL-ONLY + WEAK PURITY [decision 10]: a module holds only `type`/`let`; a `let` whose typed RHS RUNS a command is rejected — detection walks eager positions and STOPS at lambdas (`runsCommandT`), so a paramless `let x = git …` (an ECmd) is rejected but a param-ful `let f r = git …` is a deferred function, allowed (bare builtin value refs like `ls` are lazy seqs, also allowed). Enforced BOTH on import (`loadModule`, first error) and on direct `weir check <module>` (decision 10: checkable in isolation). ERRORS, each named + own message: self-import ("a file cannot import itself"), not-a-module (names the file + fix), running-a-module ("a module declares; it does not run…", detected by the marker up front in `run`), nested-import (single-hop block), literal-only path, script-only (`-e`/REPL — no file to resolve against). Eval: `CImport` replays the module body in a fresh module venv (Self.scriptPath = module's; process facts ride from the entry), exposing bindings as `alias.member`; no caching (session 3). checkStatement gained an `ImportLoader` param (5 call sites; the real loader in run/check, `scriptOnlyImport` for -e/REPL, a blocking loader inside `loadModule`). SESSION 2 (multi-file diagnostics, decision 13 completed): a module-CONTENT error (a type error, decl-only or weak-purity violation inside the module) now reports at the module's OWN file:line:col, PLUS an "imported here" NOTE (a new `note` severity) at the import line in the importer; an import-STATEMENT error (self-import, missing file, not-a-module) still reports at the import line with no note. `loadModule` returns a structured `ImportError` (`File` option — Some=content error at the module site, None=statement error at the import line — + Line/Col/Message) instead of a baked string; `StmtDiag` gained `File`/`Note` fields; `analyzeLines` emits the module diagnostic under its own File and a second `note` diagnostic at the import line; `run` renders both; `check --json` already carried a `file` field per diagnostic (`writeDiag`), now populated per-file. Per-file translate holds by construction — a module's positions were already computed in the module's own `checkStatement` context (session 1). SESSION 3 (the graph): TRANSITIVE imports — a reached module's own imports resolve too (session-1's single-hop nested-import BLOCK is removed); `loadModuleCached` threads a `cache` (normalized abs path -> LoadedModule) so a DIAMOND's shared module is checked ONCE and re-used (re-aliased per import site via a new `LoadedModule.NaturalName`), and a `chain` (the DFS path of importing files) so a repeat is a CYCLE — a check error naming the loop by file name (`a.weir → b.weir → a.weir`), same detector as self-import, different rendering (decision 9), reported at the closing edge with the note at the entry's import. Path identity is the normalized absolute path (symlinks unresolved, session 1). NO RE-EXPORT (decision 3): a module exports only its OWN types (computed from its Body's `type` decls, not the env diff — which would leak transitively-imported types) and its own values (imported members live under `Modules[·]`, never `Values`, so they were already excluded); a module USES an imported module's types internally freely, but an importer sees a member mentioning a third module's type as opaque unless it also imports that module (decision 8's discouraged-but-legal case). Eval: `replayModule` evaluates a module's Body — including NESTED `CImport`s (exposing the nested module's members as `alias.member` for this module's own lets) — recursively; no eval-time caching (a diamond's shared module re-evaluates per importer, safe because modules are pure decl-only). SESSION 4 (the LSP, decision 14): PER-URI publishing — the server groups diagnostics by their `File` and publishes each under its own `file://` URI (a module's error lands on the module's file even if unopened; the "imported here" note on the importer), deduped by position+message across the direct and dependency analyses (richer span wins), and clears a file that went clean. BUFFER-OVER-DISK — a new `Script.importSourceOverride` (a `(path -> string list option) option ref`, the `Check.toPhys` pattern) makes `loadModuleCached` read an imported file through it; the server sets it to open-buffer-then-disk (an unsaved dependency checks against what the user sees). | pipe-rhs-decides | 2026-07-29 | THE PIPE RULE — the RIGHT-HAND SIDE decides the glyph: `|` when the stage is a program or a reifier (command grammar), `|>` when it is a function. One table, symmetric, no exceptions: `cmd\|cmd`, `expr\|cmd` (the stdin feed), `cmd\|reifier` take `|`; `cmd\|fn`, `expr\|fn` take `|>`. THE ONE REMOVAL: `|` before an expression/function stage — `git rev-parse HEAD \| Seq.head` stops parsing; the spelling is `|>`. That removal makes the rule total and FIXES THE ASYMMETRY that produced the receipt (today you could pipe a command into a function with `|` but not a value — `[1;2;3] \| Seq.length` errored — a directional exception with no user-statable justification); now both halves error symmetrically. REIFIERS STAY `|` (the THIRD case, not an exception): a reifier terminates a command chain and produces a value — command grammar, not a function applied to output — so `git diff --quiet \| succeeds`, never `\|> succeeds`. ADAPTERS ARE FUNCTIONS → `|>` (`from json`/`from porcelain`/`to json` are `seq<_> -> seq<_>`; they already parse as expression stages, so the new rule requires `|>` for them automatically — no special-casing). IMPLEMENTATION: `pipeSepSpanned` captures the glyph + span; `foldChain` checks it against the RIGHT-hand seg kind (`requiredPipeOp`) and anchors the teaching error ON the glyph [D:anchor-before-read] — this catches the command-CHAIN forms (`cmd \| fn`, `cmd \|> cmd`, `cmd \|> reifier`). The value-headed operator form `expr \|> cmd` is caught by a post-parse walk (`pipeToCommand`, resolver-aware: an `EPipe` whose RHS is headed by an external command), anchored on the offending program. (4) keys attempts/delay/timeout= (retry), timeout/interval= (poll); UNBOUNDED IS UNREPRESENTABLE, not refused — no key spells forever, attempts=0 raises the floor; backoff= PARKED (additive later by construction — the key form is why parking beats guessing its shape); duplicate keys = located parse error ON the second spelling; order free. RAISES PROPAGATE — retry retries on the predicate, never exceptions (catching would swallow bugs; the reifiers make failure data). | seq-pfirst | 2026-08-05 | SEQ.PFIRST — THE RACE, unparked from tasks-underneath's recorded shape (a race, not a retry, so it lives with its Seq siblings). THE CONTRACT: the first arm to SUCCEED wins — not the first to complete — so losers' failures are swallowed BY CONSTRUCTION (a loser that dies was going to be discarded either way); the winner claim is one Interlocked.CompareExchange, then every other arm's RaceGroup is condemned BEFORE the result is released, so partial output cannot leak past the return. THE KILL MECHANISM: Session.RaceGroup — an AsyncLocal group joined at enterRace; Proc registers every spawned child into the ambient group (a no-op outside pfirst), Condemn tree-kills all registered and a condemned-flag kills LATE registrations, sealing the register-vs-condemn race window. CANCELLATION IS COOPERATIVE, stated honestly: the kill reaches PROCESSES (what weir arms actually wait on — a killed child makes its waiting arm fail fast, swallowed); a pure-compute loser finishes in the background and its result is discarded; loser threads are never aborted. Runs on the tasks-underneath pool shape (LongRunning workers, ceiling 64 shared, index pull gated on a won flag so unstarted arms never spawn after a win); pfirstWith n for family symmetry (degree<1 raises the family message). ALL-FAILED rethrows the first error BY INPUT ORDER (the fan-out convention, deterministic); EMPTY raises 'pfirst: empty sequence' — a race with no contestants has no winner. THE FIX SHAPE: definitionTarget (path, lines, pos) -> (target file option, line, col, len) — a cross-file target RE-ANALYZES the target file's lines through the import channel (buffer-over-disk), the same stateless discipline as every request; definitionFor stays as the single-file pin surface. SIGNATURES = the same fix, one file kind over: SigInfo gained SigPath/Version/SubRecords (it carried only Tool+DeclLine — the resolved path died inside loadSigs); the reference-site shape is an argv word: cmdSurfaceAt mirrors the unknown-flag check's TECmd + reified-spine recovery, the flag resolves sub-record → kebabFlag/explicitShorts reverse → field. HEAD hover = identity + partial/exhaustive + sig path + RECORDED version (loadSigs never spawns — pinned with the tool OFF PATH, where the head only warns cmd-not-found); flag hover = field type + /// doc from the sig file (the local-field format). HOVER format law kept: module member = annotated signature (the builtin rendering, params off the module's typed Body; zero-param value renders name-and-type, unnamed function degrades to the arrow) + blank + doc. URIS: a definition target rides the CLIENT's own URI when the file is open (Seq.tryFind over docs by DECODED path), pathToUri only for never-named files; FOUND+FIXED the read-direction twin — importSourceOverride looked buffers up by pathToUri(abs), so a dependency open under a client spelling (%6C…) silently read DISK; now matched by uriToPath (the mirror-bug class, decision applied both directions). INVALIDATION holds trivially (nothing retained; pinned: editing the module buffer refreshes the importer's hover). COMPLETION REPORT: Lib. offers member NAMES (never blocked) but no docs — did not come free (the fix was not retention); future session if wanted. Non-claim NOT widened: hover/definition read the import+sig set only; SECURITY.md now names #sig files (stale since the signatures session); editors.md scope line was staler still (denied all file reads) — both trued. | Lsp.fs definitionTarget/hoverAt + helpers + handler URIs + override fix; Script.fs SigInfo{SigPath,Version,SubRecords}+sigInfosForFile+targetSourceLines; Tests LSP cross-file (7); lsp-e2e cross-file section; docs/editors.md; SECURITY.md; NOTES |

A plain tuple-vs-seq (`a, b`) keeps the bare message (near-miss pinned). SIBLING POSITIONS surveyed: `let`-destructure and `for`-binder catch a refutable tuple/cons EARLIER as 'this pattern can fail; use match', routing the user to a match where the hint fires — so no extra hint owed there. STALE-MEMBER grep: no toMs/toS/toMillis in messages (the didYouMean-prefix reversion already cleaned them). | secret | 2026-08-06 | SECRET — a marker the renderers respect, NOT storage/memory protection. One session. THE FEATURE IS THE COVERAGE: a trivial type (a plain string wrapper) whose value is that EVERY renderer refuses it — a Secret leaking through Log.info or a shown containing record is not partial, it is a false promise. THE TYPE: non-generic TSecret/VSecret wrapping a string; Secret.of (ASSERT secrecy — the SAFE direction, for computed secrets; laundering is reveal, not of), Secret.map (keeps a derived value secret — `Secret.map (fun t -> "Bearer " + t)` where reveal-then-concat would defeat the type), Secret.reveal (the ONE guarded exit — the audit is the call site). NON-GENERIC RULING (3 reasons): the asymmetry (adding secrecy always safe, removing is the auditable reveal) is FLAT not conditional; a record with one secret field + three public is the common case, so make FIELDS secret not the record; a structured-secret receipt would answer 'make the fields secret' anyway (parked). THE FIVE SITES + ONE ESCAPE: show → *** (formatWith, the ONE recursive renderer, so a Secret inside a shown record/union/tuple/seq is *** BY CONSTRUCTION — the containing-record case, the likeliest naive miss, pinned FIRST); interpolation REFUSES naming reveal (a hole is how a secret reaches a log line — show renders ***, reveal uses the value); to json/to yaml REFUSE naming reveal (the unit-types non-representability posture); print REFUSES (printArgTy — show s for ***, reveal to print the value); REPL echo safe (echoValue routes through formatWith); reveal is the exit. ARGV RULING: a Secret SPLICES to argv in the CLEAR (scalarString reveals) — `curl -H $auth` is what the type exists for; ps-visible, a STATED non-claim, not silent (deliberate). CLASSES: Eq admitted FLAT (did the token change? — constant-time NOT claimed, weir is not crypto); Ord REFUSED (sorting secrets is meaningless). PRODUCERS: Env.load (env is THE CI secret channel — secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN — the MAIN producer, wraps at the boundary), Args.load (ps-visible, same non-claim), File.readSecret (one member — a mounted k8s/docker secret IS a file; whole content, trailing newlines trimmed; a family would be parked). [<Default>] on a Secret field REFUSED (a hardcoded default secret is the anti-pattern the type replaces). IN-MEMORY, stated up front: a plain string in a managed GC'd heap — NOT zeroed/pinned/encrypted; a core dump/debugger/swap sees it. SECURESTRING/ZEROING PRE-REFUSED (3 reasons, .NET guidance VERIFIED live via dotnet/platform-compat DE0001): (1) Microsoft say don't use it for new code and it is unencrypted off .NET Framework — theatre on Linux/macOS (DPAPI); (2) the value must be plaintext at every use (argv, header, hash) so it is decrypted at each use — the window that matters; (3) immutable strings cannot be zeroed in a GC'd runtime. FS0025-INVISIBLE lesson realized: 0 warnings on incremental build HID the two exhaustive-match gaps (formatWith, validateTy) — --no-incremental surfaced them; and the value-side WILDCARD danger (scalarString `| v -> unreachable`) would have CRASHED a spliced Secret — probed, VSecret→raw added. FOUND+FIXED: adding secretMembers polluted the BARE ALIASES (bare `map` collided with Seq.map → 22 unrelated tests failed) — Secret joins Option/Float as qualified-only. SECURITY.md non-claim list (incl the in-memory bullet) BEFORE any protective doc; PLAN-http AMENDED (Http.auth takes a Secret, show renders ***, send is the deliberate reveal); GUIDE token-from-env idiom. | Types.fs TSecret; Eval.fs VSecret + formatWith *** + scalarString argv-reveal + loader arms; Check.fs class Eq/Show + splice Hole-refuse/CmdArg-allow + printArgTy + json/yaml refusals + loadable + validateTy; Parser.fs type name; Argv.fs badArgsShape + Default refusal; Builtins.fs secretMembers + File.readSecret + docs + bare-alias exclusion; Tests secretTests (8, containing-record first); SECURITY.md; PLAN-http; GUIDE; NOTES; e2e readSecret trio [D:pins-walk]; Tests+e2e [<Default>]-on-Secret refusals [D:walk-findings] |
| http | 2026-08-06 | HTTP — the typed request boundary, session 1 (the core). RECONCILED two plans (08-01 draft + 08-05 devops-gap); where they disagreed this ruled. THE TRIGGER (not convenience): a typed body cannot reach the wire through curl without flag arcana that SILENTLY MANGLES bytes — `-d @-` strips newlines (form-encoding in a JSON costume), `--data-binary @-` is one flag away with nothing erroring between. THE SHAPE: a record + one runner, ZERO new grammar. Http.send : HttpRequest -> HttpResponse (ordinary builtin); the request built with the defaults-template pattern (Http.defaults, retry/poll's 2nd customer — nothing shared needed). RULED OUT (recorded closed): builder combinators (every field needs a named function — the Http.basic naming problem), key=value sugar (the examples killed it — wins at 1-2 fields, WORSE at 5-6 once a value is an application needing parens; retry/poll's ceremony for a pair of braces), a district/body-block (the body is a VALUE not a block). CONSEQUENCE: field names are PUBLIC API, named deliberately. THE BODY DESIGN (the plan left it open with a `?`): HttpBody = NoBody | Json of seq<string> | Text of string — MONOMORPHIC, NOT the generic `Json of 'a`, because weir has no Jsonable CLASS to constrain a generic case (the closed erased Eq/Show/Ord set) and a generic union in a record with a polymorphic defaults + a per-case constraint is heavy checker work; instead the caller serializes with the EXISTING `to json` (`body = Json (payload |> to json)`) — the jsonable law reused for free, no new type list, Json/Text just tag the content-type. So NO new Ty, NO bespoke node, NO generic records — the whole feature is prelude types + ordinary builtins. AUTH IS A UNION not a function family: NoAuth | Bearer of Secret | Basic of string * Secret — Basic is an ENCODING (base64(user:pass)) the runner does, which a generic 'set Authorization' op cannot and a user would get wrong; adding a scheme = adding a case (no Http.digest debate); the field survey behind two cases (Bearer dominates OAuth2/JWT/GitHub; Basic covers registries/CI; everything else is X-API-Key/PRIVATE-TOKEN custom headers or AWS SigV4 signing, NOT Authorization — so Http.header is correct for those, not a fallback). SECRET landed changes the surface: the draft's `$"Bearer {tok}"` is now ILLEGAL (interpolation refuses Secret) — the point; the auth union carries the Secret WHOLE, send applies the scheme, no call site concatenates a token; secretHeaders: seq<string * Secret> for credential headers; show masks the request's Secret FOR FREE (formatWith recursion — HttpRequest is a record containing an auth union containing a Secret; CONFIRMED not re-implemented, pinned); the value reaches the socket in the clear at send (the one deliberate reveal, argv analogue, a stated non-claim). POSTURE: status is DATA (a 404 binds, never raises — the `| complete` posture; pinned), transport failure RAISES (contracts fetch's message shapes reused, not a third); 30s timeout DEFAULT mandatory (the version without one is the CI hang — ruled over the draft's defer). Methods: all 8 as HttpMethod cases (Get..Query), send maps via HttpMethod(string) so QUERY needs no .NET-10-specific API. REIFIERS over HttpResponse DEFERRED with a reason (analysis kept): reifiers are SPAWN-AND-REIFY fused (the impls call Proc.completeWith) and pure PARSE MARKERS (succeeds is not a nameable value — unbound), hard-matched to ECmd in foldChain; only succeeds/orFail map (exitCode has no analogue, complete is redundant — HttpResponse IS the record); the clean generalization needs a RUNTIME-witnessed 'success predicate' class which breaks the erased-class invariant (weeks, own bless), the cheap version is a hard-coded Completed+HttpResponse pair saving one line over resp.status — so the deferral reason is BENEFIT/COST not tractability; Http.fetch + .status cover it. curl | from json STAYS the documented plain-GET read. REJECTED+closed: hack(HttpRequestMessage->unit), fetchTask/fetchAsync (pmap covers it — pinned), FETCH_DEBUG env-magic, per-header ops, a second JSON door. AOT: HttpClient already linked for contracts, ~0 dependency bytes (no NuGet ref added — BCL). Map PARKED (headers stay seq<string*string>) with two receipts (HTTP headers; from json into Map<string,T>) — needs Ord keys (weir's first constrained container), literal syntax question, show ordering. SESSION 2: remaining methods incl Query docs-paired-with-retry (idempotent by RFC 10008), Http.fetch, query-string builder, insecure w/ loud name. | Http.fs (send/basicToken, primitives-only); Prelude 5 types; Builtins httpMembers (defaults/send) + auth/body/header translation; Tests httpTests (5); e2e offline echo-server block (mangling/status/auth/pmap/transport/check-silent); SECURITY 5 non-claims; PLAN-http session-1-done + reifier-law proposal + Map park; GUIDE/COMING-FROM/SKILL; NOTES |

| closed-classes | 2026-08-06 | WEIR'S CLASSES ARE STRUCTURAL AND CLOSED — the principle, stated once, that keeps refusing a family of requests. The class set (Eq/Show/Ord) is compiler-owned, inferred STRUCTURALLY (no declared instances), and FULLY ERASED (a constraint never reaches the value domain, no runtime dictionaries). So any feature needing DECLARED PER-TYPE INSTANCES is refused, and the EXPLICIT SPELLING is the answer. The instances that keep arriving and keep getting this ruling: a Jsonable class to constrain a generic `Json of 'a` HTTP body (refused — the caller's `to json` is the explicit spelling [D:http]); a runtime-witnessed 'success predicate' class to reify over HttpResponse (refused — the deferral reason for reifiers-over-values [D:http]); a `Disposal` class so `within` could take user resource types (refused — within's kinds are a closed table [D:within-kinds]); anonymous records for `from json` (refused — a declared record type is the spelling [D:modules-v1 era]); a `default` keyword / per-type defaults (refused — `Http.defaults`/`Retry.defaults` are explicit record VALUES [D:retry-poll, D:http]). The through-line: weir buys its no-runtime-dictionaries simplicity by making the user NAME the thing — a type to decode into, a value to template from, a union case to add — rather than declaring an instance the compiler dispatches. A feature request of the form 'let types opt into behaviour X via a declared instance' is answered 'name it explicitly'; this row is so that answer is not re-derived per request. | (principle row — no code; cited by [D:http], [D:secret], [D:within-kinds]) |

Three collisions, same shape (Secret.map stole bare `map`, 22 unrelated ls/sortBy tests failed naming a module they never mentioned; Http.head stole `head`; Option/Float were earlier rounds): a blocklist made every NEW module unsafe by default — the only reason there was no fifth incident is that File/Dir/Path/Env/Duration/Size/Retry/Poll happen to lack hot-path-named members, and a collision on a rarer alias could ship SILENTLY (blast radius is corpus-frequency: Secret.map broke 22 tests, Http.head broke 4). THE RULING: bareAliasModules = {Seq, Str} exactly — RULED, not inherited (the pre-inversion homes map was verified 100% Seq/Str-homed, so the ruling matches reality). ZERO MOVEMENT, verified two ways: the homes map is byte-identical before/after (20 aliases), and the usage enumeration found every bare name in real use (where/first/map/sum/trim/startsWith/join across 2 REPL + 2 #loose e2e fixtures, the loose-env unit corpus, and SKILL's prose mention) homes to Seq/Str; the .weir corpus's `head`/`force` hits are user BINDINGS in strict scripts (shadowing, not aliases). THE PROPERTY PIN, not just the outcome: bareEntriesOf/bareAliasHomesOf factored over the table (public), pinned that a hypothetical Evil module with map/head/trim members contributes NOTHING while Seq keeps the alias — the fourth instance is now impossible, not unlikely; and the allowlist SET itself is pinned = {Seq, Str}, so widening is a deliberate test edit, never a side effect. FAILURE MODE CONFIRMED TEACHING: a non-allowlisted bare member gets the moved-into-a-module error (single home: use 'Secret.reveal'; multi-home: lists all candidates). | exit-hook | 2026-08-06 | THE EXIT HOOK — temp dirs survive a hard exit no more. TWO CUSTOMERS, the argument for building it (neither alone justified the machinery): (1) the PRE-EXISTING leak every script had — within tmp's cleanup is a managed finally, so Ctrl-C/kill always leaked; (2) the one pfirst ADDED, worse in KIND — a losing arm killed mid-finally when the process exits right after the winner leaks under NORMAL uninterrupted completion, no signal involved (Probe B, deterministic). THE MECHANISM: REGISTRATION, NOT SCANNING — the hook removes only directories THIS process created and still considers live (Session.liveTmpDirs, per-process by construction; a temp-root sweep of weir-tmp-* would delete a CONCURRENT weir's scratch — the sharpest correctness constraint, PINNED with two processes: one dies, the other's dir survives). The clean path deregisters after its own delete, so the hook is a BACKSTOP that normally has nothing to do. Installed LAZILY on first registration — the shebang path never pays (timing gates unchanged: 10/35/16ms). THE COVERAGE MATRIX (POSIX, live-verified): normal exit ✓ (AppDomain.ProcessExit — customer 2, Probe B now a regression pin); SIGINT ✓ + SIGTERM ✓ (PosixSignalRegistration, both; sweep then default termination); SIGKILL ✗ unreachable by definition — the residual, stated in SEMANTICS+SECURITY with the weir-tmp- prefix as the identifiability answer. Windows: ProcessExit ✓, Console.CancelKeyPress arm compiled (the POSIX Create throws there — the SIGWINCH guard's lesson [D:windows-v1]); untested until the Windows walk. HARNESS FINDING: a POSIX shell WITHOUT job control starts background jobs with SIGINT IGNORED, and .NET honors inherited SIG_IGN (the nohup convention) — so a `kill -INT` from a set -e harness is a no-op BY DESIGN, real Ctrl-C (default disposition) takes the handler (verified interactively); the e2e pins the signal path via SIGTERM, which exercises the identical sweep. SCOPE: temp dirs ONLY — within cd/env are AsyncLocal state that dies with the process (nothing on disk); Dir.newTempDir exempt BY CONTRACT (outlives the scope; its doc's stale 'neither cleans on Ctrl+C' half fixed); NOT a defer/trap feature arriving by the back door (that stays a predicted receipt). Reentrancy: the hook tolerates already-gone dirs (the double-delete pin's territory) and never throws during exit (per-dir try/catch); the loser-cleanup pin (finally runs when the process outlives it) UNCHANGED. Registrations are ROOTED (a collected PosixSignalRegistration stops handling). | Session.fs liveTmpDirs/register/deregister/installExitHook/sweep; Eval.fs within-tmp register+deregister; Builtins newTempDir doc; e2e exit-hook block (Probe B regression + TERM sweep + two-process isolation); SEMANTICS cooperative-cancellation paragraph REWRITTEN (the fixed leak no longer stated as a leak; SIGKILL residual stated); SECURITY temp-dir bullet; NOTES matrix |

MACOS: /etc/hostname was a fixture LINUX-ISM (not a GNU-ism — /etc/hosts exists on every POSIX); the unit HANG gets localization (detailed logger — Expecto prints nothing until done, so a hang was silence) + step/job timeouts; identity named in round 4: the survivors POSITIVE CONTROL carried its marker in a shell COMMENT (`sh -c "sleep 30 # marker"`) — macOS bash-as-sh EXECs a lone simple command, replacing sh and vaporizing the argv-borne marker (Linux dash kept the process, hiding the defect); portable spelling = marker as `$0` plus a compound suffix (`sh -c 'sleep 30; :' marker`) so no shell execs. Separator-agnostic fs pins (name-not-separator, weirPath'd Dir.list rows). Round 4: the e2e argv-literal WITNESS lied on Windows — MSYS binaries (Git Bash echo.exe) glob-expand `*` args received from native parents, so `$(echo "*")` convicts the witness, not weir; the witness is now a weir CHILD (`Self.args |> print` via PATH-resolved `weir`), native on every platform. THE GENERAL CASE MOSTLY DOES NOT APPLY: readability-at-extraction weir already provides AT THE BINDER (`(year, month)` names captures without naming them in the pattern); edit-insulation needs the extraction to live away from the pattern, and a weir binder list sits adjacent in the same arm; \\k backrefs work positionally and substitution is sed's job. THE DECLINED THIRD OPTION, recorded so it is not re-proposed: renumbering to match other engines — owning a mapping layer over .NET's numbering that breaks on the construct nobody tested, to buy a feature the binder already provides. THE IMPLEMENTATION IS ONE SITE: compileRegex (the single compile path — the Regex pattern, Str.isMatch/rmatch/rmatchAll all flow through it), detection by THE ENGINE'S OWN GetGroupNames (a non-numeric name = named group) — so lookbehind `(?<=`/`(?<!` and non-capturing `(?:` CANNOT false-positive (they create no groups; the hand-rolled-scan risk the plan flagged dissolves), and both spellings (`(?<x>` and `(?'x'`) reject through one table. THE DESIGN: `within proc srv = <command>` + block — the SCOPE IS THE LIFETIME: every exit (normal and raise) tree-kills and reaps; nested scopes LIFO; pmap workers hold their own; the exit hook gains processes beside tmp dirs (killed BEFORE the dirs — a spilling child holds its spill dir open on Windows). THE AWAIT IS `poll watch=srv` — a HEAD KEY, never an options field (a live handle is per-call, not configuration; the record form deliberately cannot spell it — resolving the plan's sugar probe by construction): a watched child DYING fails the poll IMMEDIATELY with code + its last output (' — last output: a ⏎ b'), and a plain TIMEOUT stamps the watched state — the enrichment only poll's own exhaustion message can carry, the reason watch is a key and Proc.await is not a member. retry+watch refuses with the reason (retry retries FAILURES; a dead process is not transient). The seq<string> abstraction CANNOT carry a partial (ReadLine upstream), so the fix is a chunk-level relay: Proc.streamSegmentsOf reads chars, onText flushes content immediately, onBreak marks terminators with EXACTLY ReadLine's split (\n, \r, \r\n) — the segment stream IS linesOf's lines with immediacy, so content is byte-identical and TIMING IS THE ONLY DIVERGENCE, tty-only (redirected keeps the batched path; pinned by od on the redirected bytes). THE RULINGS: (1) the binary guard WEAKENS, stated — the echo buffers 4 KiB AND 100ms before emitting anything (small-and-fast outputs keep the never-leak guarantee whole; the timer matters — a prompt is 20 bytes then silence, a size-only threshold holds it forever); past either bound each chunk is still checked and the stream STOPS on a NUL — a BOUNDED leak, the residue in SECURITY's register (kill -9's shape). (2) A mid-stream raise: unflushed buffer suppressed (matches the batched path's nothing-shown), already-streamed lines cannot be unshown — the footer is suppressed and the ERROR carries "(stream raised after N echoed line(s))". (3) Reifiers and captures are UNAFFECTED BY LAW — | complete is in-memory capture, $() never streams (pinned: a captured run's output appears only at completion). Silent acceptance is wrong under any ruling. THE CORRECTION: an env var name is no more the author's to choose than a JSON key — a leading digit, a dash, or a reserved word (`TYPE`, `IMPORT`) are all legal in an environment and none is a legal weir field name, so `Env.load` has the SAME wire-key problem as `from json` and the original plan missed it. `Args.load` is the opposite case and REFUSES: argv is weir's OWN boundary, not a foreign document's, so there is no wire to match — flags are DERIVED (`dryRun` -> `--dry-run`) and `[<Short "C">]`/`[<NoShort>]` already own the naming, so a Wire there would be a fourth naming mechanism for a surface with three. THE DETAILS, each decided: the refusal lands at the ARGS.LOAD CALL SITE (in `Argv.fieldProblems`, where the argv policy already lives), NOT at the declaration — a Wire-carrying record is perfectly legal and the same type may serve Env.load and the adapters; NAMES STAY EXACT — weir applies NO case mapping, because only enum VALUES are case-insensitive at this boundary and adding a third rule was refused; the OBSERVABLE is PLATFORM-SPLIT and the first Windows run found it — `[<Wire "my_var">]` does not match `MY_VAR` on POSIX but DOES on Windows, whose environment block is case-insensitive at the OS level. weir's lookup is exact on both; the difference is the platform's, and the pin is TWO-SIDED so neither half can be 'fixed' away. C14: the single-arm claim VERIFIED both paths (prompt drawn on the tty while stdout pipes, value reaches the pipeline; spontaneous exit-130 raises naming 130, statements after the fault never run) — a real ^C in a SCRIPT pipeline aborts at the fault 10/10 via the GROUP SIGINT (weir dies -2 with the tool, the same user-visible abort by a different mechanism than "raise"). CONCURRENT arms on one tty: A FINDING, not a doc sentence — the kernel distributes the BYTES of one typed line across simultaneous readers (PICKED-oe / PICKED-ntwo: "one" split o/n/e between arms), 4/10 garbled, 5/10 whole by luck, 1/10 one-arm-got-both; the docs claim single-arm ONLY and should stay that way. F1 REPORTED-NOT-FIXED: a bare script foreground child that ignores INT+HUP SURVIVES weir's signal death 10/10 (control with default dispositions: 0) — weir's signal path never kills the bare foreground child; scoped children are tree-killed, bare ones ride the group signal or nothing — adjacent to the no-orphan law, which covers scopes. TWO FATES AT THE PROMPT, both measured: the tty ^C key clears the line and the session survives; a DELIVERED SIGINT kills it 10/10 — and an inherited SIG_IGN is HONOURED (the nohup courtesy). INSTRUMENTS SIX AND SEVEN, both toward a pass, both caught by controls: pty-run.py DISCARDED an early child's real exit ("EXIT timeout" for a child dead at 1ms — its own control caught it); and a non-interactive shell's `&` bequeaths SIGINT=SIG_IGN through exec, so the B3 probe reported "weir survives SIGINT" for the harness's own reason (bisected to the preamble; the instrument now resets dispositions in the child). | range-slice-costing | 2026-08-19 | RANGE SLICING COSTED, PARKED CHEAP-BUT-CONVENTIONALLY-COSTLY — and the exploration found a bigger cost than the convention one it was sent to price. PHASE 0, three answers: (1) the indexer interior is ALREADY a general expression (`xs[1+1]` = 30) and `1..2` is simply not an expression outside `[]` (standalone `let r = 1..3` is a parse error), so `xs[1..4]` backtracks to APPLICATION of `xs` to a list literal — the misleading "not a function taking 1 argument" with the caret on the TARGET; the grammar work is therefore small and local, the favourable answer. (2) `zz[0]` on a string is REJECTED ("expected seq, got string") — and the reason is architectural: `xs[i]` is a PURE PARSE-TIME DESUGAR to a hidden `|seqItem i xs`, so there is no indexer AST node, no checker arm, and NO TYPE DISPATCH AT ALL. The string arm is not an extension of an existing dispatcher; there is no dispatcher. (3) `m[k]` is refused but NOT with the teaching SKILL promises ("no m[k] indexing — Map.get is the spelling"): the desugar forces Map into seq position and the user gets a raw unification error — a documented teaching that does not exist. THE DOMINANT COST IS ARCHITECTURE, then convention: making `zz[1..4] : string` and `xs[1..4] : seq<'a>` work under one syntax needs a real AST node plus a checker arm that resolves the target type and THEN picks the implementation — the language's FIRST type-directed desugar. Seq-only avoids it entirely but then the feature does not serve its own motivating example (a string). PER-SITE: grammar ~0.2 (one interior alternative; indexExpr is already deepen-wrapped so depth-coverage inherits, the record-pattern precedent); dispatch 0.3 seq-only vs 1-1.5 with strings; laziness free seq-only (skip/take are lazy) and two impls behind one syntax otherwise; `_[1..4]` FREE (the `_[0]` shorthand already wraps whatever the index desugars to, probed); fmt FREE (verified: `xs[1]` roundtrips — fmt is layout-based, not AST-reprinting); grammars ~0 in-repo (micro/tmLanguage position-blind, tree-sitter external since [D:ts-split]). TOTAL: seq-only 0.5-0.75 session, string+seq 1.5-2. THE TWO RULINGS, both answered so the estimate is actionable: INCLUSIVE end, forced by internal consistency rather than population preference — `[1..3]` is already an inclusive GENERATOR (probed: 3 elements), so an exclusive slice would put two readings of `1..4` inside the same bracket. TRUNCATE at both ends, because the empirical finding is THREE behaviours not two: `Seq.skip` past the end RAISES, `Seq.take` past the end TRUNCATES, `xs[i]` RAISES — so a naive skip|>take slice raises on an out-of-range START and truncates on an out-of-range END, arriving by accident rather than decision; raising on the end needs a length probe that forces the seq and breaks the pinned laziness property. Truncation diverges from `xs[i]` and the honest framing is that one element is an ASSERTION while a window is a FILTER — defensible, but it IS the second convention, on top of `..` reading as accessor-inside-`[]`-after-an-expression vs generator-inside-`[]`-alone. THE TEACHING ALTERNATIVE ~0.1 SESSION (one checker arm, one message, one fence) and it is RECOMMENDED INDEPENDENTLY of slicing, because today's diagnostic is actively misleading — the caret lands on the target and names an application the user did not write. THREE SEPARATE FINDINGS, none fixed here: (a) FSharp.Core's RAW exception text reaches weir users — `Seq.skip` emits "tried to skip {0} {1} past the end of the seq" with the generic message spliced mid-sentence, `Seq.item` emits "seq was short by N elements (Parameter 'index')"; attributed to FSharp.Core 10.1.302 by its own resource strings AFTER a first read wrongly blamed weir's own composition — weir writes its own messages everywhere else (`File.size: no such file: …`), so these are off-register, and any raise/truncate ruling needs weir-owned text; (b) [D:not-weir-shape]'s D5 suppression MISSES at least two paths — `xs.[0]` and standalone `1..3` both leak "Unknown Error(s)" plus the backtracking note, so it is a class, not an `.[`-specific bug; (c) the Map-indexing teaching SKILL documents is absent. WIDTH vs DEPTH is the new distinction: depth-coverage's gate (reference graph minus deepen-guarded nodes must be acyclic) is a statement about recursion STRUCTURE and cannot see unbounded recursion over a BOUNDED-DEPTH structure — the guard counts nesting, the crash counts leaves. STACK-DEPENDENT, which is how it hid: the depthDiagnoses harness runs on a Task.Run 1MB thread where a 20000-node walk overflows; a roomier main-thread stack does not — so #85's CI merged green while `tools/fuzz.weir` (→ 1MB thread) crashed, exposed by the observed-count e2e row at seed 4242. THE FIX: an explicit work-stack (heap, O(nodes), pop-order immaterial since the caller Set-izes) — stack-independent, provably overflow-proof at any width. MEMBERSHIP SWEEP (report-back): checkPattern/binderShape/isIrrefutablePat are the sibling per-element pattern walks — protected in practice because the parse depth guard bounds pattern NESTING (a width probe over seed 4242 found ZERO patterns exceeding 600 nodes reaching the walk; direct deep-pattern checks hit the guard at ~500); patLeafNames was the lone outlier because it runs during the parse ATTEMPT, transiently exposed before the guard rejects. THE GENERATOR GAP (report-back): Grammar.fs generates NO nested patterns — only SLet name-binders and single-level `Ctor x` arms; record patterns (#85) landed with ZERO generative fuzz coverage, so the whole pattern axis is fixture-only. The pat-cons fixture (from #50) is the deterministic guard; seed 4242 pinned in the nightly + the e2e observed-count row + a direct unit pin (analyzeLines on a 20000-node cons — reaching the assertion IS the pin, since an overflow aborts the runner). | generator-overflow | 2026-08-19 | THE UNMASKED METAMORPHIC FAILURE IS THE GENERATOR, NOT THE ASSEMBLER — [D:pattern-width]'s crash was hiding it (main aborts the fuzz suite before completing; the fix let it run, and 3 fresh seeds showed ~8/47 metamorphic failures). CHARACTERISED, not assumed: filtered to the output-neutral invariants at seed 771349 count 10000 — 8 failures, ALL 'base program rejected (rc=1): integer overflow', ZERO 'transform changed behavior'. So every assembler transform is CORRECT; the base PROGRAM overflows at runtime and the harness needs base rc=0 (error-message line numbers shift under blank insertion, so it cannot compare erroring bases). THE CAUSE is cross-statement VALUE growth in the generator: `let vN = v(N-1) * v(N-1)` squares each statement -> 99^(2^N) past int64; more statements (higher endSize/count) -> overflow, which is why 1500 passes and 10000 fails. weir's overflow DETECTION is correct — the generator was producing genuinely-invalid programs and claiming validity. THE HYPOTHESIS was right in spirit (accumulation across statements) but the accumulation is in generated VALUES, not assembler state — the assembler was never at fault. THE FIX: genIntExpr threads a value bound (intCap=1e9; `+` splits the budget, `*` takes its sqrt, a var/field is admitted only where the budget covers its cap) so every generated int <= 1e9 by construction and no reuse can overflow. STRUCTURE-PRESERVING: the assembler invariants test shape not magnitude, so `var * var` (excluded to hold the bound) costs no coverage — its EBin rendering is identical to any other. Pinned smoke + seed 771349 clean at 10k after the fix. | Grammar.fs genIntExpr + VInt dispatch; the characterisation (0 transform-changed, 8 overflow) recorded |

| install-truncation | 2026-08-19 | INSTALL.SH IS A main() INVOKED ON THE LAST LINE — the one real curl|sh objection, answered. A connection dropped mid-download leaves a complete-but-SHORTER script that the shell runs top-to-bottom up to the cut; `set -eu` cannot catch it (nothing failed), and any future edit that moves a side-effecting line earlier makes it worse silently. Wrapping the whole body in `main() { … }` with `main "$@"` last means a truncated fetch is an UNCLOSED FUNCTION — a syntax error that defines and runs nothing. install.ps1 needs no equivalent: `irm` buffers the whole response and throws on an incomplete read, so `iex` never sees a partial string. THAT LAST CLAIM IS REASONING, NOT MEASUREMENT — it follows from irm's documented buffering, we did not rig a truncated-response test against it; standard and near-certain, stated as unverified so nobody reads it as a rigged pin. PINNED offline (no network, no published release needed): a mid-`main()` head of install.sh fails `sh -n` (syntax error — the cut is computed past the opening brace so it stays mid-body as the header grows), the full template parses clean. | install.sh main() wrapper; e2e truncation-guard cells |
| install-checksum-scope | 2026-08-19 | THE INSTALLER CHECKSUM IS INTEGRITY, NOT TAMPER PROTECTION — said honestly, and two message defects fixed. SHA256SUMS is served from the SAME release origin as the binary, so an attacker who can alter one alters both; the verification catches TRUNCATION and CDN corruption, genuinely worth having, and the old comment ("worse than no installer") overclaimed. Corrected in both scripts + docs. TWO fixes rode along: (1) a MISSING SHA256SUMS entry is now NAMED ("no checksum for $name in SHA256SUMS") instead of letting empty input reach `$SUM -c` and print "no properly formatted lines" — install.ps1 already did this, install.sh now mirrors it; (2) install.ps1's PATH note used a SUBSTRING match (`-notlike "*$dest*"`) that a prefix entry falsely satisfied — now a segment match (`-split ';' -contains $dest`), the segment-vs-prefix class [D:path-under] rule 4 guards. THE TWO-ORIGIN OPTION IS TAKEN, BEFORE THE FIRST TAG — retrofitting it later is unrecoverable (users bookmark the URL; flipping always-newest→pinned after people have scripts is a silent behavior change), so it is decided while nobody depends on it. THE SHAPE: the repo install.sh/install.ps1 become TEMPLATES (`@WEIR_TAG@`/`@WEIR_SHA256SUMS@` placeholders); ci/gen-install.weir (a weir script — the scripting policy; runs the release binary against itself in publish) fills in the tag and embeds THAT release's SHA256SUMS; the generated artifact is served from weir.sh, a DIFFERENT origin than the GitHub binaries, so verification never fetches from the binary's origin — tamper-EVIDENT here, not merely integrity. The script PINS one version (re-fetch weir.sh for newer). NAMING MATTERS: the repo file is the template, the served copy is the artifact — the header, INSTALL.md, and this row all say so, or the next person edits the template expecting it to be what users fetch. TEMPLATE GUARDS: a `main()`-internal `case "$TAG" in *@*)` refuses to run unsubstituted (a real tag never contains '@'), and the SUMS placeholder sits in a heredoc so template AND artifact both `sh -n`-parse. GH ATTESTATION RODE ALONG (one workflow line, same pass): actions/attest-build-provenance signs each binary's provenance; the installer runs `gh attestation verify` best-effort (skipped when gh absent — the embedded checksum is the primary anchor, this is a second independent origin). CANONICAL URL MOVED to weir.sh/install.{sh,ps1} in both scripts + README + INSTALL.md (raw.githubusercontent named a URL the site won't serve — a third copy of "where the script lives", now one). OFFLINE-PINNED: e2e generates against a synthetic SHA256SUMS and proves the artifact parses, carries no leftover placeholders, and its embedded checksum matches the real binary; the end-to-end download→install path stays untestable until the first real release + weir.sh serving the asset (stated, not hidden). | install.sh + install.ps1 templates; ci/gen-install.weir; release.yml generation + attestation + osx-x64; docs/INSTALL.md; README; e2e generation cells |
